#### New Popular Front

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=2024_French_legislative_election&action=edit&section=11 "Edit section: New Popular Front")\]

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e5/R%C3%A9partition_des_circonscriptions_du_Nouveau_Front_populaire_%28NFP%29.svg/500px-R%C3%A9partition_des_circonscriptions_du_Nouveau_Front_populaire_%28NFP%29.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:R%C3%A9partition_des_circonscriptions_du_Nouveau_Front_populaire_(NFP).svg) Map of constituencies by the primary party affiliation of [New Popular Front](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Popular_Front "New Popular Front") candidates[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1e/Affiche_Nouveau_Front_populaire_Votez.jpg/250px-Affiche_Nouveau_Front_populaire_Votez.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Affiche_Nouveau_Front_populaire_Votez.jpg) New Popular Front campaign poster

Leftist politician [François Ruffin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fran%C3%A7ois_Ruffin "François Ruffin") called on all left-wing parties, including [The Ecologists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Ecologists_(France) "The Ecologists (France)") (LE), to form a " [popular front](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Popular_front "Popular front")" in order to avoid the "worst" outcome.[&#8202;](#ref-back-101-0)[\[77\]](#refnote-101) Calls for unity were also shared by [Socialist Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist_Party_(France) "Socialist Party (France)") (PS) leader [Olivier Faure](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olivier_Faure "Olivier Faure"), LE leader [Marine Tondelier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marine_Tondelier "Marine Tondelier") and [French Communist Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_Communist_Party "French Communist Party") (PCF) leader [Fabien Roussel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fabien_Roussel "Fabien Roussel").[&#8202;](#ref-back-102-0)[\[78\]](#refnote-102) A letter of 350 intellectuals (including [Esther Duflo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Esther_Duflo "Esther Duflo") and [Annie Ernaux](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Annie_Ernaux "Annie Ernaux")) calling for a union of left-wing forces was published in _[Le Monde](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Le_Monde "Le Monde")_ on 10 June.[&#8202;](#ref-back-103-0)[\[79\]](#refnote-103) The [New Popular Front](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Popular_Front "New Popular Front") was established on the same day, bringing together [La France Insoumise](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_France_Insoumise "La France Insoumise") (LFI), the PS, LE, the PCF, [Place Publique](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Place_Publique "Place Publique"), and various other political forces.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Garnier-0)[\[80\]](#refnote-Garnier)

On 13 June, LFI, the PS, LE, and the PCF reached an agreement on how to allocate 546 constituencies (including metropolitan France and French voters living abroad) between candidates of their choice, obtaining 229, 175, 92, and 50 constituencies, respectively, with these seats divided among themselves and allied forces.[&#8202;](#ref-back-105-0)[\[81\]](#refnote-105)[&#8202;](#ref-back-106-0)[\[82\]](#refnote-106) After outcry from other members of the alliance, [Adrien Quatennens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adrien_Quatennens "Adrien Quatennens"), previously convicted of domestic violence, withdrew his candidacy in [Nord's 1st constituency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nord's_1st_constituency "Nord's 1st constituency") on 16 June.[&#8202;](#ref-back-107-0)[\[83\]](#refnote-107) Several incumbent LFI deputies critical of leader Mélenchon – [Alexis Corbière](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexis_Corbi%C3%A8re "Alexis Corbière"), [Raquel Garrido](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raquel_Garrido "Raquel Garrido"), [Hendrik Davi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hendrik_Davi "Hendrik Davi"), and [Danielle Simonnet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Danielle_Simonnet "Danielle Simonnet") – were not renominated in their constituencies under the banner of the New Popular Front, a decision critiqued both by their supporters and other party leaders within the alliance. Nevertheless, the four candidates maintained their candidacies against LFI opponents in their constituencies. [Frédéric Mathieu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fr%C3%A9d%C3%A9ric_Mathieu "Frédéric Mathieu"), another Mélenchon critic within LFI, was also not renominated and opted not to run for re-election.[&#8202;](#ref-back-108-0)[\[84\]](#refnote-108)

The coalition unveiled its campaign platform on 14 June, which included overturning Macron's [pension](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_French_pension_reform_law "2023 French pension reform law"), unemployment, education, immigration, police, [guaranteed minimum income](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Revenu_de_solidarit%C3%A9_active "Revenu de solidarité active"), and [universal national service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Service_national_universel "Service national universel") reforms, as well as his cuts to funding for [low-income housing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HLM "HLM") and his merger of French nuclear safety organisations; lowering the retirement age to 60 in the longer-term; implementing price freezes on essential food, energy, and gas; raising the [minimum wage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minimum_wage "Minimum wage") to €1,600 per month (representing a 14% increase) and personalised housing assistance by 10%; moving towards a 32-hour work week for arduous or night shift jobs; conditioning government support for businesses on adherence to environmental, social, and anti-discriminatory regulations; reserving workers one-third of seats on boards of directors; increasing [financial transaction taxes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Financial_transaction_tax "Financial transaction tax"); banning bank financing for fossil fuels; nationalising control over water; reforming the [generalised social contribution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Generalized_Social_Contribution "Generalized Social Contribution") and inheritance taxes (capping the latter), as well as nearly tripling the number of income tax brackets from 5 to 14, to make them more progressive; re-instituting a [solidarity tax on wealth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solidarity_tax_on_wealth "Solidarity tax on wealth") "with a climate component"; enacting an exit tax on funds withdrawn from the country; charging a [vehicle miles traveled tax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vehicle_miles_traveled_tax "Vehicle miles traveled tax") on imports; guaranteeing a price floor for agricultural products; cancelling the [Comprehensive Economic and Trade Agreement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Comprehensive_Economic_and_Trade_Agreement "Comprehensive Economic and Trade Agreement") and any future free trade treaties; and forbidding the imports of agricultural products which do not meet domestic social and environmental standards.[&#8202;](#ref-back-LMNFP-0)[\[85\]](#refnote-LMNFP)

Other key NFP proposals included raising the image and salaries of public healthcare, education, justice, and government jobs; strengthening the industrial sector in key strategic areas; establishing the right to [menstrual leave](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Menstrual_leave "Menstrual leave"); prohibiting new major highway projects; outlawing [intensive animal farming](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intensive_animal_farming "Intensive animal farming") and the usage of all [PFASs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Per-_and_polyfluoroalkyl_substances "Per- and polyfluoroalkyl substances"), [neonicotinoids](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neonicotinoid "Neonicotinoid"), and [glyphosate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glyphosate-based_herbicides "Glyphosate-based herbicides"); re-examining the [Common Agricultural Policy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Common_Agricultural_Policy "Common Agricultural Policy"); providing partial or full government financing for home insulation; creating free public water fountains, showers, and toilets; constructing 200,000 new public housing units per year; requiring mandatory rent control in high-rent areas; introducing [proportional representation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Proportional_representation "Proportional representation"); removing [article 49.3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Article_49_of_the_French_Constitution#Commitment_of_responsibility_on_a_bill_(49.3) "Article 49 of the French Constitution") from the constitution; outlawing the usage of [blast balls](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blast_ball "Blast ball") by riot police; continuing to supply weapons to defend Ukraine; [recognising](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_recognition_of_the_State_of_Palestine "International recognition of the State of Palestine") the [state of Palestine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_of_Palestine "State of Palestine") along with [Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel "Israel"); and demanding compliance with the [International Court of Justice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Court_of_Justice "International Court of Justice") (ICJ) order against Israel and ceasing support for [Benjamin Netanyahu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benjamin_Netanyahu "Benjamin Netanyahu")'s government.[&#8202;](#ref-back-LMNFP-1)[\[85\]](#refnote-LMNFP)

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5b/Jean-Luc_M%C3%A9lenchon_2022_%28cropped%29.jpg/250px-Jean-Luc_M%C3%A9lenchon_2022_%28cropped%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Jean-Luc_M%C3%A9lenchon_2022_(cropped).jpg) [Jean-Luc Mélenchon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jean-Luc_M%C3%A9lenchon "Jean-Luc Mélenchon") in 2022

Opponents of the New Popular Front exploited uncertainty around who would be appointed prime minister in the event of the victory of the left, warning of the threat of [Jean-Luc Mélenchon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jean-Luc_M%C3%A9lenchon "Jean-Luc Mélenchon")'s appointment given his refusal to recuse himself from the post; although on 22 June he said that he would be willing to be appointed prime minister, he claimed that he would "not impose" himself, even as numerous other potential appointees' names have circulated.[&#8202;](#ref-back-110-0)[\[86\]](#refnote-110) Other figures on the left, while reluctant to address the question of who they believed should be prime minister, were taken aback by his comments: former president [François Hollande](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fran%C3%A7ois_Hollande "François Hollande"), running in [Corrèze's 1st constituency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corr%C3%A8ze's_1st_constituency "Corrèze's 1st constituency"), opined that Mélenchon should "keep his mouth shut," former prime minister [Lionel Jospin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lionel_Jospin "Lionel Jospin") said that he was hearing "just about everywhere, and particularly from voters of the left" that "Jean-Luc Mélenchon is not the solution," Fabien Roussel released a statement saying that "Mélenchon's nomination for the post of prime minister, \[&#8202;](#ref-back-111-0)[speculation about which\] he himself is feeding into, has never been subject of an agreement between the forces of the Popular Front," Marine Tondelier, interviewed on LCI about Mélenchon's remarks, painted a generic portrait of the attributes of the ideal prime minister, ending with "and lastly, someone who unites."[\[87\]](#refnote-111)[&#8202;](#ref-back-112-0)[\[88\]](#refnote-112) In a pre-election Elabe poll, only 16% of respondents – including just 49% of Mélenchon's 2022 voters, 24% of supporters of green parties and 17% of Socialist Party supporters – indicated they would be supportive of his appointment as prime minister, compared to 83% against the idea.[&#8202;](#ref-back-113-0)[\[89\]](#refnote-113)

Infighting broke out into the open on 24 June, starting with PCF leader [Fabien Roussel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fabien_Roussel "Fabien Roussel")'s comments in the morning, "I say this to Jean-Luc Mélenchon: no one can proclaim himself Prime Minister," to which he added that the New Popular Front needed "the most unifying personality" to lead them in the incoming National Assembly which, according to him, would clearly not be Mélenchon,[&#8202;](#ref-back-114-0)[\[90\]](#refnote-114) comments also echoed by Faure.[&#8202;](#ref-back-115-0)[\[91\]](#refnote-115) Those remarks were followed by Tondelier declaring that Mélenchon would not be prime minister, and that any prime minister would have to be chosen by consensus between the forces of the New Popular Front, but she was almost immediately rebuked by LFI national coordinator [Manuel Bompard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manuel_Bompard "Manuel Bompard"), who argued that "nobody can decide to exclude" Mélenchon.[&#8202;](#ref-back-116-0)[\[92\]](#refnote-116) In back-to-back evening interviews on [France 2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/France_2 "France 2"), [Place Publique](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Place_Publique "Place Publique") co-founder and MEP [Raphaël Glucksmann](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rapha%C3%ABl_Glucksmann "Raphaël Glucksmann"), echoed Tondelier's comments in declaring definitively that "Mélenchon will not be prime minister," even as Mélenchon told Hollande to "shut up" in response to his comments the previous day, complained that the speculation was due to "jealousy" of others on the left, and lamented the fact that he had to cede 100 additional constituencies to PS candidates compared to the [New Ecological and Social People's Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Ecological_and_Social_People's_Union "New Ecological and Social People's Union") (NUPES) in 2022 because Glucksmann's [list](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Party-list_proportional_representation "Party-list proportional representation") outpolled the LFI list in the preceding European elections.[&#8202;](#ref-back-117-0)[\[93\]](#refnote-117) Interviewed on 26 June, Faure said Mélenchon would not be prime minister, and the latter chided his alliance partners' "petty" bickering and reaffirmed that any decisions about who would become prime minister would only be made after the elections, but did not close the door to the possibility of him seeking the post, saying "there are those who don't like me and others who do like me."[&#8202;](#ref-back-LPFaure-0)[\[94\]](#refnote-LPFaure) On 1 July, LFI deputy [Sophia Chikirou](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sophia_Chikirou "Sophia Chikirou") declared that "it will be either Mélenchon at Matignon, or another" LFI member if they constitute a majority of left-of-centre elected officials, owing to her feeling that other members of the alliance were indebted to them.[&#8202;](#ref-back-119-0)[\[95\]](#refnote-119)

#### Ensemble

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=2024_French_legislative_election&action=edit&section=12 "Edit section: Ensemble")\]

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/32/Gabriel_Attal_2023_%28cropped%29.jpg/250px-Gabriel_Attal_2023_%28cropped%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Gabriel_Attal_2023_(cropped).jpg) [Gabriel Attal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gabriel_Attal "Gabriel Attal") in 2023

The [Ensemble](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Together_(coalition) "Together (coalition)") coalition of [Renaissance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renaissance_(French_political_party) "Renaissance (French political party)"), the [Democratic Movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_Movement_(France) "Democratic Movement (France)") (MoDem), [Horizons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Horizons_(political_party) "Horizons (political party)"), the [Union of Democrats and Independents](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_of_Democrats_and_Independents "Union of Democrats and Independents") (UDI), and the [Radical Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radical_Party_(France) "Radical Party (France)") was renewed after swift negotiations soon after the [dissolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dissolution_of_parliament#France "Dissolution of parliament") announcement.[&#8202;](#ref-back-120-0)[\[96\]](#refnote-120)[&#8202;](#ref-back-LP0625-0)[\[97\]](#refnote-LP0625)

Just after the early election was called, Secretary General of Renaissance [Stéphane Séjourné](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St%C3%A9phane_S%C3%A9journ%C3%A9 "Stéphane Séjourné") announced that the [presidential majority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidential_majority "Presidential majority") would field candidates against "other republican candidates" in the hopes of splitting his opposition, with [Clément Beaune](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cl%C3%A9ment_Beaune "Clément Beaune") naturally excluding [La France Insoumise](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_France_Insoumise "La France Insoumise") (LFI) and the [National Rally](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Rally "National Rally") (RN) from that definition.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Sejourne-1)[\[20\]](#refnote-Sejourne) The alliance ultimately chose not to field candidates in 67 constituencies, many of which were represented by incumbents of [The Republicans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Republicans_(France) "The Republicans (France)") (LR), and several others from the [Socialist Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist_Party_(France) "Socialist Party (France)") (PS) as well as members of the [Liberties, Independents, Overseas and Territories](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberties,_Independents,_Overseas_and_Territories "Liberties, Independents, Overseas and Territories") (LIOT) group in the [National Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Assembly_(France) "National Assembly (France)").[&#8202;](#ref-back-122-0)[\[98\]](#refnote-122) On 12 June, [Emmanuel Macron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emmanuel_Macron "Emmanuel Macron") said that he had called the election to prevent a far-right victory in the [2027 presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2027_French_presidential_election "2027 French presidential election"). He criticised The Republicans for its potential alliance with the RN, as well as the [New Popular Front](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Popular_Front "New Popular Front") (NFP), and urged all parties "able to say no to extremes" to unite.[&#8202;](#ref-back-123-0)[\[99\]](#refnote-123)

In an open letter published on 23 June, Macron wrote that he hoped that "the future government \[&#8202;](#ref-back-124-0)[would gather\] republicans of diverse sensibilities who will be known for their courage to oppose the extremes," in acknowledgement of the possibility of a post-election coalition. Additionally, in response to speculation that he might resign, he affirmed that he would remain president until May 2027.[\[100\]](#refnote-124) Most – but not all – constitutional experts rejected the possibility of Macron resigning in order to avoid potential legislative deadlock in the event of an unclear election outcome (with legislative elections prohibited within a year of the preceding one), considering that article 6 of the [constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_France "Constitution of France") explicitly prohibits presidents from serving more than two consecutive terms, and such a scenario would entail him seeking a third given that his current term would consider to have ended after such a resignation.[&#8202;](#ref-back-125-0)[\[101\]](#refnote-125)

On 20 June, Prime Minister [Gabriel Attal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gabriel_Attal "Gabriel Attal") pledged to lower electricity bills and inheritance taxes, link pensions to inflation, and provide aid to first-time property buyers.[&#8202;](#ref-back-126-0)[\[102\]](#refnote-126) Other proposals he presented included raising the value-sharing bonus by up to €10,000 per year, constructing 14 new nuclear reactors, banning access to social networks to those aged 15 and under, halving the usage of pesticides by 2030, and doubling the army budget by 2030.[&#8202;](#ref-back-127-0)[\[103\]](#refnote-127) Echoing the RN's proposals in response to a spate of youth violence, Attal also announced that he would seek to abolish age as a mitigating circumstance for statutory penalties by default, meaning that judges would charge lawbreaking children as adults unless they provided explanations as to why an exception should be granted. At the same time, he attacked the RN's programme of "division, hate, and stigmatisation," and said the RN's backtracking on various economic policies showed that they were "not ready to govern."[&#8202;](#ref-back-128-0)[\[104\]](#refnote-128) Macron likewise castigated the "uninhibited racism or anti-Semitism" of the campaign in response to RN deputy [Roger Chudeau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roger_Chudeau "Roger Chudeau") saying that his fellow former cabinet member [Najat Vallaud-Belkacem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Najat_Vallaud-Belkacem "Najat Vallaud-Belkacem") should not have been able to serve because of her dual nationality.[&#8202;](#ref-back-129-0)[\[105\]](#refnote-129)

Trailing in third place nationally behind the NFP and RN in pre-election polls, Macron and his allies decided to focus their attacks on the programme of the New Popular Front prior to the first round and mostly avoid direct confrontation against the RN until the second round.[&#8202;](#ref-back-130-0)[\[106\]](#refnote-130) Attal claimed that the NFP's proposal to raise the minimum wage by 14.3% to net €1,600 per month would lead to the loss of 500,000 jobs, and [Minister of Finance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Economics_and_Finance_(France) "Ministry of Economics and Finance (France)") [Bruno Le Maire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bruno_Le_Maire "Bruno Le Maire") claimed that it would be "a catastrophe" resulting in "mass unemployment" if implemented, with the [European Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Commission "European Commission") having just announced it would meet to launch the excessive deficit procedure against France.[&#8202;](#ref-back-131-0)[\[107\]](#refnote-131) Macron publicly denounced the NFP's "totally immigrationist" stance and decried proposals which would make it easier for transgender people to change their civil status by allowing them to do so at their local town hall as "completely grotesque,"[&#8202;](#ref-back-132-0)[\[108\]](#refnote-132) and his former prime minister [Élisabeth Borne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%89lisabeth_Borne "Élisabeth Borne") decried the alliance as being one of "separatist wokists who support Islamism and communitarianism" with a nonsensical programme and disastrous economic policies.[&#8202;](#ref-back-133-0)[\[109\]](#refnote-133)

A recurring theme of the Ensemble campaign was the willingness of its figureheads to draw equivalencies between the New Popular Front and National Rally. On 21 June, Macron argued that, "contrary to what some say," the left and RN are not "rampart\[&#8202;](#ref-back-134-0)[s\] of \[each\] other ... there are extremes we must not allow to pass."[\[110\]](#refnote-134) Finance minister Bruno Le Maire warned that a victory by either the far-right or the left could cause a financial crisis,[&#8202;](#ref-back-bbcfront-0)[\[111\]](#refnote-bbcfront) lambasting both of their economic platforms as "leftist projects inspired by Marxism."[&#8202;](#ref-back-136-0)[\[112\]](#refnote-136) In an interview on 24 June, [Gender Equality Minister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minister_for_Gender_Equality,_Diversity_and_Equal_Opportunities "Minister for Gender Equality, Diversity and Equal Opportunities") [Aurore Bergé](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aurore_Berg%C3%A9 "Aurore Bergé") remarked that "the best rampart, particularly against the Popular Front, is not the RN, it's us," and like Macron, refused to give second-round voting instructions in support of either of "the extremes" represented by the New Popular Front and RN prior to the first round.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Berge-0)[\[113\]](#refnote-Berge) In a podcast episode released the same day, Macron warned that the "two extremes" would lead France "to civil war," whether because of the xenophobia of the RN or the communitarianism of the left.[&#8202;](#ref-back-138-0)[\[114\]](#refnote-138)

Many of Macron's closest advisors publicly expressed dismay at his decision to dissolve the National Assembly in the days after his surprise announcement. [Yaël Braun-Pivet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ya%C3%ABl_Braun-Pivet "Yaël Braun-Pivet"), [president of the National Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President_of_the_National_Assembly_of_France "President of the National Assembly of France") before the dissolution, privately disagreed with the decision and attempted to dissuade him, and said she believed that a coalition was possible. Finance Minister Bruno Le Maire trashed Macron's coterie at the Élysée as "woodlice," and his former prime minister [Édouard Philippe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%89douard_Philippe "Édouard Philippe"), head of the Horizons party within the Ensemble alliance, said that Macron had "killed the presidential majority" through his reckless decision.[&#8202;](#ref-back-YBP-0)[\[115\]](#refnote-YBP) Outgoing Ensemble deputies expressed exasperation with Macron, with one remarking that "I wish he'd shut up and let us get out of the mess he's gotten us into;" [François Bayrou](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fran%C3%A7ois_Bayrou "François Bayrou"), leader of alliance member MoDem, deemed it necessary to "de-Macronise the campaign;"[&#8202;](#ref-back-140-0)[\[116\]](#refnote-140) and candidates became "fed up" with Macron's refusal to abide by his promise to stay out of the campaign.[&#8202;](#ref-back-141-0)[\[117\]](#refnote-141)

As Macron's popularity ratings plunged to their lowest level ever in post-dissolution surveys, with [Frédéric Dabi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fr%C3%A9d%C3%A9ric_Dabi?action=edit&redlink=1 "Frédéric Dabi (page does not exist)")\[ [fr](https://fr.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fr%C3%A9d%C3%A9ric%20Dabi "fr:Frédéric Dabi")\] noting that most respondents in an [Ifop](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Institut_fran%C3%A7ais_d'opinion_publique "Institut français d'opinion publique")- [JDD](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Le_Journal_du_Dimanche "Le Journal du Dimanche") survey characterised the decision as "incomprehensible," "thoughtless," or "irresponsible" and 70% in a BVA Xsight- [RTL](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RTL_(French_radio) "RTL (French radio)") survey declaring that they did not want Macron involved in the campaign,[&#8202;](#ref-back-142-0)[\[118\]](#refnote-142)[&#8202;](#ref-back-143-0)[\[119\]](#refnote-143)[&#8202;](#ref-back-144-0)[\[120\]](#refnote-144) Ensemble candidates kept a local focus, with images of Macron were almost entirely absent from campaign posters: only one out of 22 government ministers' posters featured his image.[&#8202;](#ref-back-145-0)[\[121\]](#refnote-145) Along with Philippe, Le Maire, and [Minister of the Interior](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minister_of_the_Interior_(France) "Minister of the Interior (France)") [Gérald Darmanin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/G%C3%A9rald_Darmanin "Gérald Darmanin") (who announced that he would leave the government if re-elected to the National Assembly),[&#8202;](#ref-back-146-0)[\[122\]](#refnote-146) many of Macron's early supporters distanced themselves from him,[&#8202;](#ref-back-147-0)[\[123\]](#refnote-147) and he faced increasing rejection among former allies frustrated with his antics and public statements.[&#8202;](#ref-back-148-0)[\[124\]](#refnote-148)

#### The Republicans

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=2024_French_legislative_election&action=edit&section=13 "Edit section: The Republicans")\]

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d3/%C3%89ric_Ciotti_%28cropped%29.jpg/250px-%C3%89ric_Ciotti_%28cropped%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:%C3%89ric_Ciotti_(cropped).jpg) [Éric Ciotti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%89ric_Ciotti "Éric Ciotti") in 2023

Main articles: [2024 The Republicans crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_The_Republicans_crisis "2024 The Republicans crisis") and [Union of the Far-Right](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_of_the_Far-Right "Union of the Far-Right")

The president of [The Republicans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Republicans_(France) "The Republicans (France)") (LR), [Éric Ciotti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%89ric_Ciotti "Éric Ciotti"), spoke in favour of an alliance with the [National Rally](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Rally "National Rally") (RN) during an 11 June interview with the French channel [TF1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TF1 "TF1"). [Olivier Marleix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olivier_Marleix "Olivier Marleix"), the head of the party in the [National Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Assembly_(France) "National Assembly (France)"), called for Ciotti's resignation in response.[&#8202;](#ref-back-:0-0)[\[125\]](#refnote-:0) On 12 June, The Republicans' political committee voted unanimously to remove Ciotti as its president and expel him from the party. However, the latter rejected the decision, calling it "a flagrant violation of our statutes" that was illegal and void.[&#8202;](#ref-back-f2412june-0)[\[126\]](#refnote-f2412june) A Paris court reviewed the decision on 14 June, in which Ciotti was reinstated as party leader in the interim, as well as a member of the party,[&#8202;](#ref-back-151-0)[\[127\]](#refnote-151) which was followed by two additional abortive attempts to remove him,[&#8202;](#ref-back-152-0)[\[128\]](#refnote-152) while the local branch of The Republicans in [Hauts-de-Seine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hauts-de-Seine "Hauts-de-Seine") announced a local alliance with [Renaissance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renaissance_(French_political_party) "Renaissance (French political party)").[&#8202;](#ref-back-bbcfront-1)[\[111\]](#refnote-bbcfront) On 17 June, Ciotti secured an agreement with RN to present 62 candidates (later 63), none of which are outgoing LR deputies except for himself and his close ally [Christelle d'Intorni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christelle_d'Intorni "Christelle d'Intorni"), while the national investiture committee of LR unveiled candidates in a majority of other constituencies, including all other incumbent deputies seeking re-election, as well as fielding candidates against both Ciotti and d'Intorni.[&#8202;](#ref-back-CNILR-0)[\[74\]](#refnote-CNILR)[&#8202;](#ref-back-CNI-1)[\[72\]](#refnote-CNI)[&#8202;](#ref-back-LM1-2)[\[1\]](#refnote-LM1) Because Ciotti requested that the party's bank require his authorisation for any transactions, LR candidates are not receiving any financial support from the party.[&#8202;](#ref-back-153-0)[\[129\]](#refnote-153)

With no detailed national election programme to run on, most LR candidates opted to campaign primarily on issues concerning their constituencies, rely on their strong local roots and name recognition in order to fight for their survival, and keep their distance from the drama surrounding the other three main political forces and Ciotti's alliance with the RN. Numerous incumbent LR deputies declined to feature the party's logo on their election paraphernalia, stayed out of national media, and tried to portray themselves as independent of any party, with [Aurélien Pradié](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aur%C3%A9lien_Pradi%C3%A9 "Aurélien Pradié") choosing to describe himself on leaflets as "a strong voice, a free voice,"[&#8202;](#ref-back-LRLE-0)[\[130\]](#refnote-LRLE) before announcing that he would run only under the label of his micro-party _Du courage_ on 26 June, declaring in an interview with _[La Dépêche](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_D%C3%A9p%C3%AAche_du_Midi "La Dépêche du Midi")_ that " [Gaullism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaullism "Gaullism") isn't dead, it's more alive than ever, but LR is dead."[&#8202;](#ref-back-155-0)[\[131\]](#refnote-155) This reduced visibility was also the product of the highly varied circumstances of LR candidates, with 63 invested as part of the LR-RN alliance, roughly 400 invested by the party's national investiture committee, and 39 other candidates (including 26 incumbents) completely unopposed by the [Ensemble](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Together_(coalition) "Together (coalition)") coalition owing to their "constructive" alignment with Macron's policies. Even figures with a significant national profile like former party president [Laurent Wauquiez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laurent_Wauquiez "Laurent Wauquiez"), threatened by the possibility of an RN wave, sought to stay out of the national spotlight and focused on avoiding being subsumed by the tripolarisation of the electorate.[&#8202;](#ref-back-LRLE-1)[\[130\]](#refnote-LRLE)[&#8202;](#ref-back-LM1-3)[\[1\]](#refnote-LM1)

#### National Rally

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=2024_French_legislative_election&action=edit&section=14 "Edit section: National Rally")\]

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f3/Jordan_Bardella_%28cropped%29.jpg/250px-Jordan_Bardella_%28cropped%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Jordan_Bardella_(cropped).jpg) [Jordan Bardella](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jordan_Bardella "Jordan Bardella") in 2022

Eight of the 30 [National Rally](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Rally "National Rally") (RN) [MEPs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Member_of_the_European_Parliament "Member of the European Parliament") newly elected to the [European Parliament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Parliament "European Parliament") in June 2024 decided to run in the national election. As occupying both posts is impossible, MEPs who won in the legislative elections were replaced with other party members further down the [list](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Party-list_proportional_representation "Party-list proportional representation").[&#8202;](#ref-back-156-0)[\[132\]](#refnote-156)

[Marine Le Pen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marine_Le_Pen "Marine Le Pen") promised that the RN would form a "national unity government" should it win the election,[&#8202;](#ref-back-France24-0)[\[133\]](#refnote-France24) and in an interview with _[La Voix du Nord](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Voix_du_Nord "La Voix du Nord")_, she indicated she was open to the possibility of appointments for figures from the left in an RN-led government.[&#8202;](#ref-back-158-0)[\[134\]](#refnote-158) At the same time, party leader [Jordan Bardella](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jordan_Bardella "Jordan Bardella") said that he was "the only one capable of blocking [Jean-Luc Mélenchon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jean-Luc_M%C3%A9lenchon "Jean-Luc Mélenchon") and blocking the far left" and urged "all the patriotic forces of the republic" to unite and prevent the left from winning the election. He also pledged to pass an immigration law allowing the deportation of "delinquents and Islamists" and cut energy costs as prime minister.[&#8202;](#ref-back-bbcfront-2)[\[111\]](#refnote-bbcfront) In an interview with _[Le Monde](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Le_Monde "Le Monde")_, Le Pen confirmed that Bardella would not seek the post of prime minister in the absence of an absolute majority.[&#8202;](#ref-back-159-0)[\[135\]](#refnote-159)

On 18 June, Bardella urged voters to give his party an "absolute majority" for it to be able to govern effectively, while pledging to cut energy taxes to 5.5% from 20%.[&#8202;](#ref-back-160-0)[\[136\]](#refnote-160) Bardella pledged to uphold French military commitments to [NATO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NATO "NATO") and [support Ukraine against the Russian invasion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine "Russian invasion of Ukraine"), but ruled out sending long-range missiles and other weapons that could be used to strike Russian territory.[&#8202;](#ref-back-161-0)[\[137\]](#refnote-161) Alluding to the possibility of [Emmanuel Macron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emmanuel_Macron "Emmanuel Macron") sending ground forces to Ukraine, Le Pen deemed Macron's title of "commander-in-chief of the armed forces" as "honorary" given the need for both the heads and state and government to make most defence decisions, though constitutional law experts noted that the president's approval was still required for the usage of nuclear weapons.[&#8202;](#ref-back-162-0)[\[138\]](#refnote-162)

Due to worries about public backlash and concerns from investors, the RN softened and postponed some elements of its initial economic proposals, including the planned tax exemptions on those under 30 and abolition of the [value-added tax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Value-added_tax "Value-added tax") (VAT) on 100 essential products,[&#8202;](#ref-back-163-0)[\[139\]](#refnote-163) and proposals to increase teachers' salaries were also deferred. Despite initial claims otherwise, Bardella reaffirmed on 17 June that the RN intended to repeal the [2023 pension reform](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_French_pension_reform_law "2023 French pension reform law") and reduce the legal retirement age to 60, but only for those who started working before the age of 20.[&#8202;](#ref-back-164-0)[\[140\]](#refnote-164) In an interview with _[Le Journal du Dimanche](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Le_Journal_du_Dimanche "Le Journal du Dimanche")_ published on 22 June, Bardella announced that, as prime minister, he would initiate a national budgetary audit and call a constitutional referendum to guarantee reductions in migratory flows in 2027. He also stated that he did not support [Frexit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frexit "Frexit") and assured that, after their alliance, members of [The Republicans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Republicans_(France) "The Republicans (France)") (LR) supported jointly by [Éric Ciotti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%89ric_Ciotti "Éric Ciotti") and the RN would be included in his government.[&#8202;](#ref-back-165-0)[\[141\]](#refnote-165)

Bardella officially unveiled the RN's programme on 24 June, including measures to introduce [mandatory sentencing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mandatory_sentencing "Mandatory sentencing"), end [child benefits](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Child_benefit "Child benefit") for parents of underage repeat offenders, and sentence youth criminals to short prison sentences at closed educational centres for children. He confirmed that the RN continued to intend to abolish _[jus soli](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jus_soli "Jus soli")_ because "the automatic acquisition of French nationality is no longer justified in a world of 8 billion people, \[&#8202;](#ref-back-RN2-0)[with\] our daily struggles of our inability to integrate and assimilate them multiplying on our soil," and expressed his desire to both "re-establish the offence of illegal stays" and solidify these proposals in the constitution "to also make them untouchable by European or international jurisprudence" through a national referendum. In addition, he declared that he would scrutinise "spending that encourages immigration" and "certain expensive and abusive tax loopholes," and that the reversal of Macron's pension reform would be implemented gradually, shifting the legal retirement age to 62 for those who have worked for at least 40 years.[\[142\]](#refnote-RN2) The party is against measures to tackle climate change and protecting the environment.[&#8202;](#ref-back-167-0)[\[143\]](#refnote-167)

Other RN proposals included seeking to provide incentives for medical professionals to work in underserved areas and for retirees to return to work,[&#8202;](#ref-back-RN2-1)[\[142\]](#refnote-RN2) reducing taxes on agriculture, privatising French national media, boosting fertility rates by allowing parents to claim their first two children as a full share rather than the current half-share for the purposes of personal income tax calculations, eliminating inheritance taxes for lower-income families, continuing to not [recognise Palestine as a state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_recognition_of_the_State_of_Palestine "International recognition of the State of Palestine") as doing so would, in his view, be "to recognise terrorism,"[&#8202;](#ref-back-168-0)[\[144\]](#refnote-168) imposing moratoriums on new wind farms and the closure of healthcare facilities, banning agricultural products which fall below standards for domestic products, and ensuring that only French nationals be eligible for some security and defence jobs, after an earlier announcement that [Dual nationals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Multiple_citizenship "Multiple citizenship") would be banned from such "sensitive" jobs as those.[&#8202;](#ref-back-169-0)[\[145\]](#refnote-169) After outcry following RN deputy [Roger Chudeau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roger_Chudeau "Roger Chudeau")'s comments that dual nationals (specifically [Najat Vallaud-Belkacem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Najat_Vallaud-Belkacem "Najat Vallaud-Belkacem")) should not hold ministerial posts, Le Pen disavowed the idea that of restricting ministerial posts on basis of dual nationality and added that Chudeau's comments were contrary to the RN's programme.[&#8202;](#ref-back-170-0)[\[146\]](#refnote-170)

#### Other political parties

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=2024_French_legislative_election&action=edit&section=15 "Edit section: Other political parties")\]

[Marion Maréchal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marion_Mar%C3%A9chal "Marion Maréchal"), a far-right candidate for [Reconquête](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reconqu%C3%AAte "Reconquête") in the [preceding European Parliament election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_European_Parliament_election_in_France "2024 European Parliament election in France"), met with her aunt [Marine Le Pen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marine_Le_Pen "Marine Le Pen") and [Jordan Bardella](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jordan_Bardella "Jordan Bardella"), leaders of the [National Rally](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Rally "National Rally") (RN), on 10 June in order to discuss a potential far-right alliance during the legislative election.[&#8202;](#ref-back-171-0)[\[147\]](#refnote-171) After the meeting, Maréchal indicated that Bardella was opposed to an alliance with Reconquête as his party did not want to be affiliated with Reconquête party leader [Éric Zemmour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%89ric_Zemmour "Éric Zemmour");[&#8202;](#ref-back-172-0)[\[148\]](#refnote-172) regardless, she announced her endorsement of the RN. On 12 June, Zemmour announced that he was expelling Maréchal from the party.[&#8202;](#ref-back-173-0)[\[149\]](#refnote-173) The party ultimately presented candidates in 330 constituencies, deciding not to run candidates in constituencies where ideologically similar candidates had the strongest chance of winning.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Rec1-0)[\[76\]](#refnote-Rec1)

[Debout la France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Debout_la_France "Debout la France") only contested 107 constituencies, backing candidates supported by the RN elsewhere, and party leader [Nicolas Dupont-Aignan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicolas_Dupont-Aignan "Nicolas Dupont-Aignan") expressed his support for the alliance between [Éric Ciotti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%89ric_Ciotti "Éric Ciotti") and the RN.[&#8202;](#ref-back-DLF1-0)[\[70\]](#refnote-DLF1)[&#8202;](#ref-back-DLF2-0)[\[71\]](#refnote-DLF2)

Trotskyist party [Lutte Ouvrière](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lutte_Ouvri%C3%A8re "Lutte Ouvrière") presented candidates in 550 constituencies. Other parties presenting a double-digit number of candidates, according to a _[Le Monde](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Le_Monde "Le Monde")_ analysis, include the [New Anticapitalist Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Anticapitalist_Party "New Anticapitalist Party") with 30 candidates, [Ecology at the Centre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ecology_at_the_Centre "Ecology at the Centre") with 23 candidates, [Unser Land](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unser_Land "Unser Land") with 14 candidates, and [Résistons!](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/R%C3%A9sistons! "Résistons!") with 12 candidates.[&#8202;](#ref-back-LM1-4)[\[1\]](#refnote-LM1) The [Animalist Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Animalist_Party "Animalist Party"), which was able to field candidates in 421 constituencies in 2022, announced that it would not attempt to field candidates with such short notice before the first round of the 2024 legislative election.[&#8202;](#ref-back-PA-1)[\[44\]](#refnote-PA)

### Debates

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=2024_French_legislative_election&action=edit&section=16 "Edit section: Debates")\]

[France 3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/France_3 "France 3") and [France Bleu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/France_Bleu "France Bleu") announced they would organise more than 200 debates between local candidates which would be broadcast on local television and radio with the first set held on 19 June, followed by additional debates on 26 June and 3 July.[&#8202;](#ref-back-174-0)[\[150\]](#refnote-174) Between the two rounds, 23 RN candidates refused to participate or cancelled their appearances in these debates.[&#8202;](#ref-back-LMlive3-0)[\[151\]](#refnote-LMlive3)

[TF1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TF1 "TF1") also announced plans to hold a debate on 25 June between [Gabriel Attal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gabriel_Attal "Gabriel Attal"), [Jordan Bardella](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jordan_Bardella "Jordan Bardella"), and [Manuel Bompard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manuel_Bompard "Manuel Bompard").[&#8202;](#ref-back-176-0)[\[152\]](#refnote-176) On 22 June, Attal, taking note of three-time presidential candidate [Jean-Luc Mélenchon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jean-Luc_M%C3%A9lenchon "Jean-Luc Mélenchon")'s comments refusing to rule out the possibility that he might seek to become prime minister, challenged him to participate in the debate instead of Bompard, the national operations team coordinator of [La France Insoumise](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_France_Insoumise "La France Insoumise"),[&#8202;](#ref-back-177-0)[\[153\]](#refnote-177) a demand also echoed by Bardella, though Mélenchon declined.[&#8202;](#ref-back-178-0)[\[154\]](#refnote-178) [The Republicans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Republicans_(France) "The Republicans (France)") appealed their exclusion from the TF1 debate to the _[Conseil d'État](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conseil_d'%C3%89tat "Conseil d'État")_, with the [Regulatory Authority for Audiovisual and Digital Communication](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Regulatory_Authority_for_Audiovisual_and_Digital_Communication "Regulatory Authority for Audiovisual and Digital Communication") already having declined to take action,[&#8202;](#ref-back-179-0)[\[155\]](#refnote-179) though this appeal was rejected the next day, a few hours before the debate.[&#8202;](#ref-back-180-0)[\[156\]](#refnote-180)

[France 2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/France_2 "France 2") also held a debate on 27 June between Attal, Bardella, and [Olivier Faure](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olivier_Faure "Olivier Faure"),[&#8202;](#ref-back-France2-0)[\[157\]](#refnote-France2) during which Attal directed viewers to a calculator for retirement pensions under the [New Popular Front](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Popular_Front "New Popular Front")'s plans, leading the alliance to initiate emergency proceedings against [Renaissance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renaissance_(French_political_party) "Renaissance (French political party)") for "false and misleading allegations likely to alter the vote" under article L163-2 of the electoral code, with hearings set for 1 July.[&#8202;](#ref-back-182-0)[\[158\]](#refnote-182)

Several debates were also initially anticipated between the two rounds, including one hosted by France 2 on 4 July. For the other two debates between the two rounds, the New Popular Front chose to send [Marine Tondelier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marine_Tondelier "Marine Tondelier") on [BFM TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BFM_TV "BFM TV") and [Ian Brossat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ian_Brossat "Ian Brossat") on [CNews](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CNews "CNews"), respecting the boycott of the channel by [The Ecologists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Ecologists_(political_party) "The Ecologists (political party)") and the [Socialist Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist_Party_(France) "Socialist Party (France)").[&#8202;](#ref-back-France2-1)[\[157\]](#refnote-France2)[&#8202;](#ref-back-183-0)[\[159\]](#refnote-183) On 1 July, Bardella challenged Mélenchon to a one-on-one debate, which the latter declined, and Tondelier confirmed her participation in the third debate,[&#8202;](#ref-back-LMlive2-0)[\[160\]](#refnote-LMlive2) before BFM TV announced on 2 July that no debate would be held, and the three invitees would instead each be allocated a one-hour segment in a different viewing format.[&#8202;](#ref-back-LMlive3-1)[\[151\]](#refnote-LMlive3) Later reporting indicated that this decision was motivated by the fact that The Ecologists were allocated a smaller share of constituencies in the agreement of the [New Popular Front](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Popular_Front "New Popular Front"), but [Marc-Olivier Fogiel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marc-Olivier_Fogiel "Marc-Olivier Fogiel") publicly insisted that it was the RN's stance that forced BFM TV to call off the debate.[&#8202;](#ref-back-185-0)[\[161\]](#refnote-185) Ultimately, it was announced that none of these broadcasts would be held in a debate format.[&#8202;](#ref-back-LMlive4-0)[\[162\]](#refnote-LMlive4)

### Candidate incidents and controversies

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=2024_French_legislative_election&action=edit&section=17 "Edit section: Candidate incidents and controversies")\]

#### Racism, anti-Semitism, and hate symbols

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=2024_French_legislative_election&action=edit&section=18 "Edit section: Racism, anti-Semitism, and hate symbols")\]

##### Against candidates

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=2024_French_legislative_election&action=edit&section=19 "Edit section: Against candidates")\]

[![](https://thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3b/2024-03-03_-_Congr%C3%A8s_du_PSE-_105_-_Raphael_Glucksmann.jpg/250px-2024-03-03_-_Congr%C3%A8s_du_PSE-_105_-_Raphael_Glucksmann.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:2024-03-03_-_Congr%C3%A8s_du_PSE-_105_-_Raphael_Glucksmann.jpg) [Raphaël Glucksmann](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rapha%C3%ABl_Glucksmann "Raphaël Glucksmann") in 2024

The campaign was marred by numerous incidents of [racial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Racism_in_France "Racism in France") and [anti-Semitic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antisemitism_in_France "Antisemitism in France") abuse. On 17 June, [Hanane Mansouri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hanane_Mansouri "Hanane Mansouri"), [The Republicans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Republicans_(France) "The Republicans (France)") (LR) member supported by the [National Rally](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Rally "National Rally") (RN) for [Isère's 8th constituency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Is%C3%A8re's_8th_constituency "Isère's 8th constituency"), revealed that she was inundated by [anti-Arab racist abuse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-Arab_racism "Anti-Arab racism") after her candidacy was confirmed.[&#8202;](#ref-back-187-0)[\[163\]](#refnote-187) [La France Insoumise](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_France_Insoumise "La France Insoumise") (LFI) candidate Yasmina Samri, running in [Charente-Maritime's 1st constituency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charente-Maritime's_1st_constituency "Charente-Maritime's 1st constituency"), decided to end her candidacy after receiving numerous racist insults and threats.[&#8202;](#ref-back-188-0)[\[164\]](#refnote-188) While campaigning in [Marseille](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marseille "Marseille") for the [New Popular Front](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Popular_Front "New Popular Front") on 20 June, [Raphaël Glucksmann](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rapha%C3%ABl_Glucksmann "Raphaël Glucksmann"), accompanied by journalist [Léa Salamé](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/L%C3%A9a_Salam%C3%A9 "Léa Salamé"), was recorded being told "shame on you as a Jew" by a voter after trying to give them a leaflet. He subsequently revealed that his cell phone number had been leaked on [Telegram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telegram_(software) "Telegram (software)") groups and he was now simultaneously being bombarded with hateful messages from members of the Jewish far-right – outraged at his involvement in the alliance – and those on the left who alleged he was a pro- [Netanyahu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benjamin_Netanyahu "Benjamin Netanyahu") [Zionist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zionism "Zionism") on the basis of his [Ashkenazic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ashkenazi_Jews "Ashkenazi Jews") surname.[&#8202;](#ref-back-189-0)[\[165\]](#refnote-189)

On 24 June, Shannon Seban, [Renaissance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renaissance_(French_political_party) "Renaissance (French political party)") candidate for [Val-de-Marne's 10th constituency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Val-de-Marne's_10th_constituency "Val-de-Marne's 10th constituency"), announced that she filed a police complaint in response to a group of pro- [Palestine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestine "Palestine") festivalgoers screaming "get out, dirty Zionist" at her.[&#8202;](#ref-back-190-0)[\[166\]](#refnote-190) In [Calvados](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Calvados_(department) "Calvados (department)"), the campaign posters of LFI [MEP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Member_of_European_Parliament "Member of European Parliament")-elect [Emma Fourreau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emma_Fourreau "Emma Fourreau") and [6th constituency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Calvados's_6th_constituency "Calvados's 6th constituency") candidate Noé Gauchard were defaced with swastikas and neo-Nazi symbols,[&#8202;](#ref-back-191-0)[\[167\]](#refnote-191) as were those for [The Ecologists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Ecologists_(France) "The Ecologists (France)") (LE) candidate Guillaume Hédouin in [Manche's 1st constituency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manche's_1st_constituency "Manche's 1st constituency") along with the word "Islam,"[&#8202;](#ref-back-192-0)[\[168\]](#refnote-192) while those for Pascaline Lécorché of [Place Publique](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Place_Publique "Place Publique") in [Bouches-du-Rhône's 1st constituency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bouches-du-Rh%C3%B4ne's_1st_constituency "Bouches-du-Rhône's 1st constituency") were covered with " [Hamas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hamas "Hamas") candidates" stickers.[&#8202;](#ref-back-193-0)[\[169\]](#refnote-193) On the day of the first round, [Roxane Lundy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roxane_Lundy "Roxane Lundy"), candidate for [Génération.s](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/G%C3%A9n%C3%A9ration.s "Génération.s") in [Oise's 1st constituency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oise's_1st_constituency "Oise's 1st constituency"), discovered that her campaign posters in [Beauvais](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beauvais "Beauvais") and [Maignelay-Montigny](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maignelay-Montigny "Maignelay-Montigny") had been defaced with swastikas.[&#8202;](#ref-back-194-0)[\[170\]](#refnote-194)

##### By candidates

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=2024_French_legislative_election&action=edit&section=20 "Edit section: By candidates")\]

On 17 June, _[Libération](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lib%C3%A9ration "Libération")_ reported that Marie-Christine Sorin, RN candidate for [Hautes-Pyrénées's 1st constituency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hautes-Pyr%C3%A9n%C3%A9es's_1st_constituency "Hautes-Pyrénées's 1st constituency"), made a tweet in January saying "No, not all civilizations are equal ... \[&#8202;](#ref-back-195-0)[some\] have just stayed below bestiality in the evolutionary chain."[\[171\]](#refnote-195) The RN initially suspended their support for Joseph Martin, candidate for [Morbihan's 1st constituency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Morbihan's_1st_constituency "Morbihan's 1st constituency"), after the discovery of a 2018 tweet reading "Gas brought justice to the victims of [the Holocaust](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Holocaust "The Holocaust"),"[&#8202;](#ref-back-196-0)[\[172\]](#refnote-196) but reinstated him after he explained that he meant it as an allusion to the death of [Holocaust denier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holocaust_denial "Holocaust denial") [Robert Faurisson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Faurisson "Robert Faurisson") the day prior.[&#8202;](#ref-back-197-0)[\[173\]](#refnote-197) On 19 June, the pro- [Éric Ciotti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%89ric_Ciotti "Éric Ciotti") faction of LR withdrew their support for Louis-Joseph Pecher, jointly supported by the RN in [Meurthe-et-Moselle's 5th constituency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meurthe-et-Moselle's_5th_constituency "Meurthe-et-Moselle's 5th constituency"), due to his history of "anti-Semitic, [homophobic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homophobia "Homophobia") and odious remarks."[&#8202;](#ref-back-198-0)[\[174\]](#refnote-198) Another pro-Ciotti LR candidate supported by the RN, [Gilles Bourdouleix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gilles_Bourdouleix "Gilles Bourdouleix"), was previously convicted for condoning [crimes against humanity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crimes_against_humanity "Crimes against humanity") in 2014 for saying that " [Hitler](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adolf_Hitler "Adolf Hitler") didn't kill enough" [Romani people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romani_people "Romani people"), though his sentence was suspended on the basis that he never intended for his remarks to be made public.[&#8202;](#ref-back-199-0)[\[175\]](#refnote-199) On 25 June, Pascal Schneider, mayor of [Neuves-Maisons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neuves-Maisons "Neuves-Maisons"), filed a complaint with the public prosecutor against Pierre-Nicolas Nups, candidate of the [Party of France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Party_of_France "Party of France") in [Meurthe-et-Moselle's 5th constituency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meurthe-et-Moselle's_5th_constituency "Meurthe-et-Moselle's 5th constituency"), for electoral posters featuring a young white boy with blue eyes and blond hair reading " [Let's give white children a future](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fourteen_Words "Fourteen Words")."[&#8202;](#ref-back-200-0)[\[176\]](#refnote-200)

On 26 June, [Reconquête](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reconqu%C3%AAte "Reconquête") leader [Éric Zemmour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%89ric_Zemmour "Éric Zemmour") shared a video of him tapping along to the rhythm of the [TikTok](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TikTok "TikTok")-viral song "Je partira pas" ("I willn't \[sic\] leave"), which features various overtly xenophobic lyrics, with audio of the song – remixing the screams of a man being apprehended by the police while being carried off a plane in a viral video – in the background. While the song, supposedly [created with AI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Artificial_intelligence_art "Artificial intelligence art") by an artist under the name "Crazy-Girl," was removed for violating TikTok's content guidelines, it spread widely over social media, and was denounced by [French Communist Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_Communist_Party "French Communist Party") leader [Fabien Roussel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fabien_Roussel "Fabien Roussel").[&#8202;](#ref-back-201-0)[\[177\]](#refnote-201) [SOS Racisme](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SOS_Racisme "SOS Racisme") announced that it filed a complaint with the authorities regarding the song for [incitement of hatred](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hate_speech_laws_in_France "Hate speech laws in France").[&#8202;](#ref-back-JPP1-0)[\[178\]](#refnote-JPP1) The RN also denounced the "calls for murder, violent misogyny, crude anti-Semitism and conspiracism" of the lyrics of _No Pasarán_ which was released by a collective of rappers opposed to the far-right after the first round.[&#8202;](#ref-back-LMlive3-2)[\[151\]](#refnote-LMlive3)

Thierry Dussud of the RN, [substitute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Substitute_(elections)#France "Substitute (elections)") for the Ciotti-aligned LR candidate supported by the RN in [Ardèche's 2nd constituency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ard%C3%A8che's_2nd_constituency "Ardèche's 2nd constituency"), [Vincent Trébuchet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vincent_Tr%C3%A9buchet "Vincent Trébuchet"), announced on 26 June that he would quit after racist and anti-Semitic posts he made resurfaced, including one in which he declared "Let's give the Africans back to Africa."[&#8202;](#ref-back-203-0)[\[179\]](#refnote-203) In addition to the 20 RN candidates identified by _Libération_ as having made racist, anti-Semitic, and discriminatory comments on social media, _[Mediapart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mediapart "Mediapart")_ also uncovered similar posts by another 24 RN candidates using their real names.[&#8202;](#ref-back-204-0)[\[180\]](#refnote-204) On 27 June, LFI withdrew its support for Reda Belkadi, candidate for [Loir-et-Cher's 1st constituency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loir-et-Cher's_1st_constituency "Loir-et-Cher's 1st constituency"), after the discovery of his past anti-Semitic and homophobic tweets including anti-Jewish and anti-gay slurs.[&#8202;](#ref-back-LFPCF-0)[\[181\]](#refnote-LFPCF)

On 1 July, Paule Veyre de Soras, RN candidate for [Mayenne's 1st constituency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mayenne's_1st_constituency "Mayenne's 1st constituency"), apologised for her comments that "my ophthalmologist is a Jew, and my dentist is a Muslim" in response to a journalist's question about racism within her party.[&#8202;](#ref-back-206-0)[\[182\]](#refnote-206) On 2 July, Ludivine Daoudi, RN candidate for [Calvados's 1st constituency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Calvados's_1st_constituency "Calvados's 1st constituency"), withdrew after her NFP opponent circulated images of her wearing a Luftwaffe visor cap with a swastika on it.[&#8202;](#ref-back-207-0)[\[183\]](#refnote-207) That same day, [Daniel Grenon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daniel_Grenon "Daniel Grenon"), incumbent RN deputy for [Yonne's 1st constituency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yonne's_1st_constituency "Yonne's 1st constituency"), was referred to the public prosecutor for saying during a debate that "North Africans came to power in 2016 ... \[&#8202;](#ref-back-208-0)[they\] have no place in high places."[\[184\]](#refnote-208)

After regional daily _[La Montagne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Montagne_(newspaper) "La Montagne (newspaper)")_ uncovered racist social media posts by Isabelle Dupré, RN candidate for [Puy-de-Dôme's 2nd constituency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Puy-de-D%C3%B4me's_2nd_constituency "Puy-de-Dôme's 2nd constituency"), she responded "If I'm elected, I'll stop the racist humor." Reporting also uncovered photos in which Julie Apricena, substitute to Pierre Gentillet, RN candidate for [Cher's 3rd constituency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cher's_3rd_constituency "Cher's 3rd constituency"), was accompanied by neo-Nazi skinheads and wearing a T-shirt reading "White Pride, World Wide."[&#8202;](#ref-back-LMlive3-3)[\[151\]](#refnote-LMlive3) On 3 July, Jean-Yves Le Boulanger, RN candidate for [Côtes-d'Armor's 5th constituency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/C%C3%B4tes-d'Armor's_5th_constituency "Côtes-d'Armor's 5th constituency"), denied that he was a "fascist" because [he has "friends of colour"](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/I'm_not_racist,_I_have_black_friends "I'm not racist, I have black friends") and didn't use his motorcycle to run over "a coloured priest who blessed" him.[&#8202;](#ref-back-209-0)[\[185\]](#refnote-209) The same day, Laurent Gnaedig, RN candidate for [Haut-Rhin's 1st constituency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haut-Rhin's_1st_constituency "Haut-Rhin's 1st constituency"), said that he believed [Jean-Marie Le Pen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jean-Marie_Le_Pen "Jean-Marie Le Pen")'s comments that gas chambers were a mere "detail" of World War II were not anti-Semitic, and added that he had "doubts" about Le Pen's conviction for dismissing crimes against humanity.[&#8202;](#ref-back-210-0)[\[186\]](#refnote-210) A civil servant of Moroccan origin accused Philippe Torre, RN candidate for [Aisne's 2nd constituency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aisne's_2nd_constituency "Aisne's 2nd constituency"), of making racist statements in an altercation on 3 July by implying that he would be deported despite being a French national.[&#8202;](#ref-back-211-0)[\[187\]](#refnote-211)

#### Physical attacks involving candidates and activists

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=2024_French_legislative_election&action=edit&section=21 "Edit section: Physical attacks involving candidates and activists")\]

Several candidates also reported attacks against them and activists supporting them over the course of the campaign. [Florian Chauche](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Florian_Chauche "Florian Chauche"), LFI candidate and incumbent deputy for [Territoire de Belfort's 2nd constituency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Territoire_de_Belfort's_2nd_constituency "Territoire de Belfort's 2nd constituency"), decried physical attacks and the usage of racist slurs against his supporters on 17 June.[&#8202;](#ref-back-212-0)[\[188\]](#refnote-212) On 20 June, Hervé Breuil, RN candidate for [Loire's 2nd constituency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loire's_2nd_constituency "Loire's 2nd constituency"), alleged that a group of masked individuals struck him from behind and pelted him with rotten fruit while hurling verbal abuse at him.[&#8202;](#ref-back-213-0)[\[189\]](#refnote-213) On 23 June, numerous left-wing activists (for LE candidate Céline Papin in [Gironde's 1st constituency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gironde's_1st_constituency "Gironde's 1st constituency"), LE candidate and outgoing deputy [Sabrina Sebaihi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sabrina_Sebaihi "Sabrina Sebaihi") in [Hauts-de-Seine's 4th constituency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hauts-de-Seine's_4th_constituency "Hauts-de-Seine's 4th constituency"), and [Socialist Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist_Party_(France) "Socialist Party (France)") (PS) candidate Joao Martins Pereira in [Val-de-Marne's 8th constituency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Val-de-Marne's_8th_constituency "Val-de-Marne's 8th constituency")) reported being assaulted and threatened by supporters of the far-right.[&#8202;](#ref-back-NM22-0)[\[190\]](#refnote-NM22)

Séverine Vézies, LFI candidate for [Doubs's 1st constituency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doubs's_1st_constituency "Doubs's 1st constituency"), claimed that a self-proclaimed RN supporter attempted to strike a man in his 80s with a broomstick while putting up a campaign poster for her on 25 June.[&#8202;](#ref-back-215-0)[\[191\]](#refnote-215) [Danielle Simonnet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Danielle_Simonnet "Danielle Simonnet"), LFI deputy for [Paris's 15th constituency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paris's_15th_constituency "Paris's 15th constituency"), organised a "rally against the far-right" after four of her supporters were tear-gassed, assaulted, and called "anti-Semitic bastards" by a group of far-right supporters while putting up election posters in the [20th arrondissement of Paris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/20th_arrondissement_of_Paris "20th arrondissement of Paris") on the evening of 2 July.[&#8202;](#ref-back-216-0)[\[192\]](#refnote-216) On 3 July, RN MEP [Marie Dauchy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marie_Dauchy "Marie Dauchy"), candidate for [Savoie's 3rd constituency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Savoie's_3rd_constituency "Savoie's 3rd constituency"), announced she was suspending campaigning after being attacked at the market in [La Rochette](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Rochette,_Savoie "La Rochette, Savoie"), with a merchant allegedly trying to kick her while tearing up her campaign leaflets.[&#8202;](#ref-back-217-0)[\[193\]](#refnote-217)

On the evening of 3 July, [Prisca Thevenot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prisca_Thevenot "Prisca Thevenot"), [Spokesperson of the Government of France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spokesperson_of_the_Government_of_France "Spokesperson of the Government of France") and Renaissance candidate for [Hauts-de-Seine's 8th constituency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hauts-de-Seine's_8th_constituency "Hauts-de-Seine's 8th constituency"), along with her substitute [Virginie Lanlo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginie_Lanlo?action=edit&redlink=1 "Virginie Lanlo (page does not exist)")\[ [fr](https://fr.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginie%20Lanlo "fr:Virginie Lanlo")\] and one of her supporters, were attacked by a group of fifteen people while putting up campaign posters in [Meudon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meudon "Meudon"), with four of them arrested following the attack. The supporter in question was struck in the face using a scooter, breaking their jaw, while Lanlo was punched and kicked, while Thevenot herself escaped uninjured.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Thevenot-0)[\[194\]](#refnote-Thevenot) Bernard Dupré, deputy mayor of [La Tronche](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Tronche "La Tronche"), was punched in the right eye by a man who he says claimed to be an LFI supporter on 4 July while putting up campaign posters for the campaign of [Olivier Véran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olivier_V%C3%A9ran "Olivier Véran"), Renaissance candidate for [Isère's 1st constituency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Is%C3%A8re's_1st_constituency "Isère's 1st constituency"),[&#8202;](#ref-back-219-0)[\[195\]](#refnote-219) though the attacker claimed that Dupré was the first one to strike him.[&#8202;](#ref-back-LMlive5-0)[\[196\]](#refnote-LMlive5)

On 4 July, [Thomas Mesnier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thomas_Mesnier "Thomas Mesnier"), former [Horizons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Horizons_(political_party) "Horizons (political party)") deputy and candidate for [Charente's 1st constituency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charente's_1st_constituency "Charente's 1st constituency"), reported that one of his supporters had been kicked, punched, and targeted by homophobic insults by a group of four people while putting up posters in [Angoulême](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angoul%C3%AAme "Angoulême").[&#8202;](#ref-back-221-0)[\[197\]](#refnote-221) That evening, [Geoffroy Didier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geoffroy_Didier "Geoffroy Didier"), LR candidate for [Hauts-de-Seine's 6th constituency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hauts-de-Seine's_6th_constituency "Hauts-de-Seine's 6th constituency"), reported that one of his supporters had been "violently assaulted and threatened with death" in [Neuilly-sur-Seine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neuilly-sur-Seine "Neuilly-sur-Seine") while leafleting for his campaign.[&#8202;](#ref-back-222-0)[\[198\]](#refnote-222) Three supporters of Maxime Viancin, LFI candidate for [Loire-Atlantique's 10th constituency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loire-Atlantique's_10th_constituency "Loire-Atlantique's 10th constituency"), were chased down the street by an RN supporter who screamed "Bardella will take care of the lefties, dykes and trans" at them before pushing one and punching another, while his wife joined him on the street while waving a French flag.[&#8202;](#ref-back-223-0)[\[199\]](#refnote-223)

On 2 July, _Le Courrier de la Mayenne_ resurfaced a report from 12 January 1995 about Annie-Claire Jaccoud Bell, RN candidate for [Mayenne's 3rd constituency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mayenne's_3rd_constituency "Mayenne's 3rd constituency"), engaging in an attempted armed hostage-taking at the [Ernée](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ern%C3%A9e "Ernée") town hall, firing a single wayward shot from a rifle she smuggled into the town hall while fighting with a secretary.[&#8202;](#ref-back-224-0)[\[200\]](#refnote-224)

In anticipation of potential violence after the second round, [Minister of the Interior](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minister_of_the_Interior_(France) "Minister of the Interior (France)") [Gérald Darmanin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/G%C3%A9rald_Darmanin "Gérald Darmanin") announced that 30,000 police would be deployed across the country on the evening of 7 July.[&#8202;](#ref-back-Thevenot-1)[\[194\]](#refnote-Thevenot) He also announced on the morning of 5 July that despite the short campaign, 51 different candidates, substitutes, and supporters had been physically attacked, often suffering significant injuries requiring medical attention in the process, with more than 30 arrests having been made among people with "extremely varied profiles."[&#8202;](#ref-back-LMlive5-1)[\[196\]](#refnote-LMlive5)

#### Death threats

\[ [edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=2024_French_legislative_election&action=edit&section=22 "Edit section: Death threats")\]

Many candidates also reported receiving [death threats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Death_threat "Death threat") both online and in real life. On 15 June, [Jean-Jacques Gaultier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jean-Jacques_Gaultier "Jean-Jacques Gaultier"), LR candidate for [Vosges's 4th constituency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vosges's_4th_constituency "Vosges's 4th constituency"), reported receiving a death threat via post.[&#8202;](#ref-back-225-0)[\[201\]](#refnote-225) On 19 June, Elsa Richard, LE candidate for [Maine-et-Loire's 1st constituency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maine-et-Loire's_1st_constituency "Maine-et-Loire's 1st constituency"), reported messages from people threatening to behead her in front of her house to the police.[&#8202;](#ref-back-226-0)[\[202\]](#refnote-226) On 21 June, [Pierre Morel-À-L'Huissier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pierre_Morel-%C3%80-L'Huissier "Pierre Morel-À-L'Huissier"), [miscellaneous centre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miscellaneous_centre "Miscellaneous centre") candidate and outgoing deputy for [Lozère's constituency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loz%C3%A8re's_constituency "Lozère's constituency"), filed a police complaint after discovering a large tag with a death threat against him in [Gorges du Tarn Causses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gorges_du_Tarn_Causses "Gorges du Tarn Causses").[&#8202;](#ref-back-227-0)[\[203\]](#refnote-227)[&#8202;](#ref-back-LM1-5)[\[1\]](#refnote-LM1) After being targeted by extensive harassment and numerous death threats on social media, Ethan Leys, RN candidate for [Nord's 8th constituency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nord's_8th_constituency "Nord's 8th constituency"), filed a police complaint and suspended in-person campaign activities.[&#8202;](#ref-back-228-0)[\[204\]](#refnote-228) While attending a [France 3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/France_3 "France 3") Franche-Comté debate, a supporter of Philippe Ghiles, Reconquête candidate for [Haute-Saône's 1st constituency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haute-Sa%C3%B4ne's_1st_constituency "Haute-Saône's 1st constituency"), threatened to kill the debate host, prompting the latter to file a complaint with the police.[&#8202;](#ref-back-229-0)[\[205\]](#refnote-229) [Babette de Rozières](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Babette_de_Rozi%C3%A8res "Babette de Rozières"), candidate jointly supported by Ciotti and the RN in [Yvelines's 7th constituency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yvelines's_7th_constituency "Yvelines's 7th constituency"), asserted that she was targeted with death threats and racist comments after her candidacy was unveiled.[&#8202;](#ref-back-230-0)[\[206\]](#refnote-230) After a hundred lawyers signed on to an anti-RN letter in _[Marianne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marianne_(magazine) "Marianne (magazine)")_, the far-right website _Réseau libre_ published an article calling for the murder of all of its signatories.[&#8202;](#ref-back-LMlive4-1)[\[162\]](#refnote-LMlive4)

In addition, Alice Cordier, head of the [feminist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Feminism "Feminism") and [white identitarian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Identitarian_movement "Identitarian movement") group [Collectif Némésis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Collectif_N%C3%A9m%C3%A9sis "Collectif Némésis"), filed a complaint for death threats recorded on 16 October 2023 made against her by [Raphaël Arnault](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rapha%C3%ABl_Arnault "Raphaël Arnault"), LFI candidate in [Vaucluse's 1st constituency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vaucluse's_1st_constituency "Vaucluse's 1st constituency") and a spokesperson for the [Jeune Garde Antifasciste](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeune_Garde_Antifasciste "Jeune Garde Antifasciste") with several " [S cards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fiche_S "Fiche S")" (which have often been applied to individuals considered potential threats to [national security](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_security "National security")) to his name.[&#8202;](#ref-back-231-0)[\[207\]](#refnote-231)
